    \newsavebox\pandoc@box
    \newcommand*\pandocbounded[1]{%
      \sbox\pandoc@box{#1}%
      % scaling factors for width and height
      \Gscale@div\@tempa\textheight{\dimexpr\ht\pandoc@box+\dp\pandoc@box\relax}%
      \Gscale@div\@tempb\linewidth{\wd\pandoc@box}%
      % select the smaller of both
      \ifdim\@tempb\p@<\@tempa\p@
        \let\@tempa\@tempb
      \fi
      % scaling accordingly (\@tempa < 1)
      \ifdim\@tempa\p@<\p@
        \scalebox{\@tempa}{\usebox\pandoc@box}%
      % scaling not needed, use as it is
      \else
        \usebox{\pandoc@box}%
      \fi
    }

\def\PY@do#1{\PY@bc{\PY@tc{\PY@ul{%

\def\PY#1#2{\PY@reset\PY@toks#1+\relax+\PY@do{#2}}

    % For linebreaks inside Verbatim environment from package fancyvrb.
    \makeatletter
        \newbox\Wrappedcontinuationbox
        \newbox\Wrappedvisiblespacebox
        \newcommand*\Wrappedvisiblespace {\textcolor{red}{\textvisiblespace}}
        \newcommand*\Wrappedcontinuationsymbol {\textcolor{red}{\llap{\tiny$\m@th\hookrightarrow$}}}
        \newcommand*\Wrappedcontinuationindent {3ex }
        \newcommand*\Wrappedafterbreak {\kern\Wrappedcontinuationindent\copy\Wrappedcontinuationbox}
        % Take advantage of the already applied Pygments mark-up to insert
        % potential linebreaks for TeX processing.
        %        {, <, #, %, $, ' and ": go to next line.
        %        _, }, ^, &, >, - and ~: stay at end of broken line.
        % Use of \textquotesingle for straight quote.
        \newcommand*\Wrappedbreaksatspecials {%
            \def\PYGZus{\discretionary{\char`\_}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\_}}%
            \def\PYGZob{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\{}{\char`\{}}%
            \def\PYGZcb{\discretionary{\char`\}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\}}}%
            \def\PYGZca{\discretionary{\char`\^}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\^}}%
            \def\PYGZam{\discretionary{\char`\&}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\&}}%
            \def\PYGZlt{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\<}{\char`\<}}%
            \def\PYGZgt{\discretionary{\char`\>}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\>}}%
            \def\PYGZsh{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\#}{\char`\#}}%
            \def\PYGZpc{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\%}{\char`\%}}%
            \def\PYGZdl{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\$}{\char`\$}}%
            \def\PYGZhy{\discretionary{\char`\-}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\-}}%
            \def\PYGZsq{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\textquotesingle}{\textquotesingle}}%
            \def\PYGZdq{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\"}{\char`\"}}%
            \def\PYGZti{\discretionary{\char`\~}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\~}}%
        }
        % Some characters . , ; ? ! / are not pygmentized.
        % This macro makes them "active" and they will insert potential linebreaks
        \newcommand*\Wrappedbreaksatpunct {%
            \lccode`\~`\.\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\.}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\.}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\,\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\,}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\,}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\;\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\;}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\;}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\:\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\:}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\:}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\?\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\?}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\?}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\!\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\!}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\!}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\/\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\/}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\/}}}%
            \catcode`\.\active
            \catcode`\,\active
            \catcode`\;\active
            \catcode`\:\active
            \catcode`\?\active
            \catcode`\!\active
            \catcode`\/\active
            \lccode`\~`\~
        }
    \makeatother

    \let\OriginalVerbatim=\Verbatim
    \makeatletter
    \renewcommand{\Verbatim}[1][1]{%
        %\parskip\z@skip
        \sbox\Wrappedcontinuationbox {\Wrappedcontinuationsymbol}%
        \sbox\Wrappedvisiblespacebox {\FV@SetupFont\Wrappedvisiblespace}%
        \def\FancyVerbFormatLine ##1{\hsize\linewidth
            \vtop{\raggedright\hyphenpenalty\z@\exhyphenpenalty\z@
                \doublehyphendemerits\z@\finalhyphendemerits\z@
                \strut ##1\strut}%
        }%
        % If the linebreak is at a space, the latter will be displayed as visible
        % space at end of first line, and a continuation symbol starts next line.
        % Stretch/shrink are however usually zero for typewriter font.
        \def\FV@Space {%
            \nobreak\hskip\z@ plus\fontdimen3\font minus\fontdimen4\font
            \discretionary{\copy\Wrappedvisiblespacebox}{\Wrappedafterbreak}
            {\kern\fontdimen2\font}%
        }%

        % Allow breaks at special characters using \PYG... macros.
        \Wrappedbreaksatspecials
        % Breaks at punctuation characters . , ; ? ! and / need catcode=\active
        \OriginalVerbatim[#1,codes*=\Wrappedbreaksatpunct]%
    }
    \makeatother

    \section{High-Level Overview of Direct Employment in London's Data
Centre Sector: Technical
Annex}\label{high-level-overview-of-direct-employment-in-londons-data-centre-sector-technical-annex}

\subsection{Overview}\label{overview}

link to repo: https://github.com/a-saveleva/casa0007\_project

Recently, data centres (DC) were designated as Critical National
Infrastructure in the UK, placing them alongside essential services such
as water, energy, and emergency response. According to one of our
sources (an architect involved in data centre planning and design), the
UK currently has around 500 data centres, and this number is expected to
increase by approximately 20\% over the next 5--10 years.

With a substantial number of DCs in the development pipeline, questions
are increasingly being raised about the economic benefits these
facilities bring to local areas. This document presents a step-by-step
regression modelling and clustering exercise that addresses the
following questions:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  How many data centres are there in London?
\item
  What are their basic characteristics, particularly those relevant to
  planning (e.g.~size, employment, and energy use)?
\item
  How many people are employed in data centres, and how does this
  relationship change with DC size, location, or other characteristics?
\end{enumerate}

\PY{n}{data\PYZus{}ldn\PYZus{}exstg}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{b8\PYZus{}gia\PYZus{}ha\PYZus{}per\PYZus{}site\PYZus{}ha}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{data\PYZus{}ldn\PYZus{}exstg}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{b8\PYZus{}gia\PYZus{}sqm}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{/}\PY{l+m+mi}{10000}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{/} \PY{n}{data\PYZus{}ldn\PYZus{}exstg}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{application\PYZus{}details\PYZus{}site\PYZus{}area}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{]}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} year\PYZus{}range = max\PYZus{}year \PYZhy{} min\PYZus{}year}
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} data\PYZus{}ldn\PYZus{}exstg[\PYZdq{}data\PYZus{}centre\PYZus{}since\PYZus{}scaled\PYZdq{}] = ((data\PYZus{}ldn\PYZus{}exstg[col] \PYZhy{} min\PYZus{}year) + 1)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \subsubsection{Column definitions}\label{column-definitions}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Status and ownership} - Unique PA reference - \textbf{id} - DC
postcode - \textbf{postcode} - DC address - \textbf{address} - Category
col indicating the state of completion - \textbf{completed} - Date when
DC was operational for the first time in this building. Note the size
and staff numbers reflect the current situation, not when DC opened -
\textbf{data\_centre\_since} - Year/date when the first pre-app stage
document was received - \textbf{preapp\_started} - Boolean col
indicating whether DC is enterprise or any other type -
\textbf{enterprise} - Current owner/occupier of DC -
\textbf{current\_occupier} - Company website - \textbf{website} - Unique
property reference - \textbf{uprn} - Local authority (borough) name -
\textbf{lpa\_name}. Note a few of DC in Slough were manually added as
the information became available. Slough DC will only be used for size
and staff analysis, not for the spatial clustering
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Attached planning application} - PA code (contains repetitions
with unique other columns, use id for cross-comparing) -
\textbf{lpa\_app\_no} - PA webpage - \textbf{url\_planning\_app} - PA
decision date - \textbf{decision\_date}. Note it does not always
represent a moment when the DC was created - PA description. Note it
sometimes refers to PAs with additions to DC, not its development -
\textbf{description} - Notes about the application and current use -
\textbf{notes} - PA system update - \textbf{last\_updated} - Helper
column with the data source -- \textbf{is\_full\_planning}
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Spatial parameters} - Gross internal area of the DC use -
\textbf{b8\_gia\_sqm} - Site area in hectares --
\textbf{application\_details\_site\_area} - Number of storeys in the DC
building, or stories occupied by the DC if other uses present -
\textbf{b8\_no\_storeys} - Height of the DC in meters
\textbf{b8\_max\_height\_m} - List of other uses within the building
with the DC --
\textbf{other\_use\_classes\_present\_within\_b8\_building} - Geometry
cols - \textbf{geometry\_point, geometry} -\\
\textbf{Technical parameters} - Total facility energy -
\textbf{utility\_total\_electr\_capacity\_mva} - IT equipment energy,
available for customers -- \textbf{it\_load\_capacity\_mw} - Data center
power density i - \textbf{density\_kw\_max\_per\_sqm} - Tier benchmark
-- \textbf{tier} - Power Usage Effectiveness: ratio of total facility
energy to IT equipment energy- \textbf{pue}
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Employment data} - Total staff number in the DC -
\textbf{b8\_total\_staff\_fte} - N of security staff -
\textbf{security\_staff} - N of engineering/technical staff -
\textbf{site\_engineers\_staff} - N of administrative staff -
\textbf{managers\_staff} - N of cleaning/housekeeping staff -
\textbf{cleaners\_staff} - N of visitor staff, typically client
technicians - \textbf{visitor\_staff\_not\_on\_site} - N of staff
working within one cluster, if more than one DC is in the area. Mostly
refers to admin/technicians - \textbf{cluster\_staff\_not\_on\_site} - N
of staff present on site per day - \textbf{b8\_employees\_at\_a\_day}
\end{quote}

\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{make\PYZus{}transparent}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{color}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{alpha}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mf}{0.3}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{color}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{startswith}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{rgb}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{color}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{replace}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{)}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{, }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{alpha}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{)}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{replace}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{rgb}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{rgba}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{elif} \PY{n}{color}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{startswith}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZsh{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n}{r}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{g}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{b} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb}{tuple}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb}{int}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{color}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{:}\PY{n}{i}\PY{o}{+}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{16}\PY{p}{)} \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{i} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{5}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{k}{return} \PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{rgba(}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{r}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{,}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{g}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{,}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{b}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{,}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{alpha}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{)}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{color}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} https://www.statsmodels.org/dev/examples/notebooks/generated/linear\PYZus{}regression\PYZus{}diagnostics\PYZus{}plots.html}
\PY{n}{style\PYZus{}talk} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{seaborn\PYZhy{}talk}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} refer to plt.style.available}

\PY{k}{class}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nc}{LinearRegDiagnostic}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Diagnostic plots to identify potential problems in a linear regression fit.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Mainly,}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        a. non\PYZhy{}linearity of data}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        b. Correlation of error terms}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        c. non\PYZhy{}constant variance}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        d. outliers}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        e. high\PYZhy{}leverage points}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        f. collinearity}

\PY{l+s+sd}{        (1) Fixed incorrect annotation of the top most extreme residuals in}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            the Residuals vs Fitted and, especially, the Normal Q\PYZhy{}Q plots.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        (2) Changed Residuals vs Leverage plot to match closer the y\PYZhy{}axis}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            range shown in the equivalent plot in the R package ggfortify.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        (3) Added horizontal line at y=0 in Residuals vs Leverage plot to}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            match the plots in R package ggfortify and base R.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        (4) Added option for placing a vertical guideline on the Residuals}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            vs Leverage plot using the rule of thumb of h = 2p/n to denote}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            high leverage (high\PYZus{}leverage\PYZus{}threshold=True).}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        (5) Added two more ways to compute the Cook\PYZsq{}s Distance (D) threshold:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            * \PYZsq{}baseR\PYZsq{}: D \PYZgt{} 1 and D \PYZgt{} 0.5 (default)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            * \PYZsq{}convention\PYZsq{}: D \PYZgt{} 4/n}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            * \PYZsq{}dof\PYZsq{}: D \PYZgt{} 4 / (n \PYZhy{} k \PYZhy{} 1)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        (6) Fixed class name to conform to Pascal casing convention}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        (7) Fixed Residuals vs Leverage legend to work with loc=\PYZsq{}best\PYZsq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}

    \PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf+fm}{\PYZus{}\PYZus{}init\PYZus{}\PYZus{}}\PY{p}{(}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{n}{results}\PY{p}{:} \PY{n}{Type}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{statsmodels}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{regression}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{linear\PYZus{}model}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{RegressionResultsWrapper}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{o}{\PYZgt{}} \PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        For a linear regression model, generates following diagnostic plots:}

\PY{l+s+sd}{        a. residual}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        b. qq}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        c. scale location and}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        d. leverage}

\PY{l+s+sd}{        and a table}

\PY{l+s+sd}{        e. vif}

\PY{l+s+sd}{        Args:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            results (Type[statsmodels.regression.linear\PYZus{}model.RegressionResultsWrapper]):}
\PY{l+s+sd}{                must be instance of statsmodels.regression.linear\PYZus{}model object}

\PY{l+s+sd}{        Raises:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{            TypeError: if instance does not belong to above object}

\PY{l+s+sd}{        Example:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} import numpy as np}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} import pandas as pd}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} import statsmodels.formula.api as smf}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} x = np.linspace(\PYZhy{}np.pi, np.pi, 100)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} y = 3*x + 8 + np.random.normal(0,1, 100)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} df = pd.DataFrame(\PYZob{}\PYZsq{}x\PYZsq{}:x, \PYZsq{}y\PYZsq{}:y\PYZcb{})}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} res = smf.ols(formula= \PYZdq{}y \PYZti{} x\PYZdq{}, data=df).fit()}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} cls = Linear\PYZus{}Reg\PYZus{}Diagnostic(res)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} cls(plot\PYZus{}context=\PYZdq{}seaborn\PYZhy{}v0\PYZus{}8\PYZhy{}paper\PYZdq{})}

\PY{l+s+sd}{        In case you do not need all plots you can also independently make an individual plot/table}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        in following ways}

\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} cls = Linear\PYZus{}Reg\PYZus{}Diagnostic(res)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} cls.residual\PYZus{}plot()}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} cls.qq\PYZus{}plot()}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} cls.scale\PYZus{}location\PYZus{}plot()}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} cls.leverage\PYZus{}plot()}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZgt{}\PYZgt{}\PYZgt{} cls.vif\PYZus{}table()}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}

        \PY{k}{if} \PY{p}{(}
            \PY{n+nb}{isinstance}\PY{p}{(}
                \PY{n}{results}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{statsmodels}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{regression}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{linear\PYZus{}model}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{RegressionResultsWrapper}
            \PY{p}{)}
            \PY{o+ow}{is} \PY{k+kc}{False}
        \PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
            \PY{k}{raise} \PY{n+ne}{TypeError}\PY{p}{(}
                \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{result must be instance of statsmodels.regression.linear\PYZus{}model.RegressionResultsWrapper object}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}
            \PY{p}{)}

        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{results} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{maybe\PYZus{}unwrap\PYZus{}results}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{results}\PY{p}{)}

        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{y\PYZus{}true} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{results}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{model}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{endog}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{y\PYZus{}predict} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{results}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{fittedvalues}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{xvar} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{results}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{model}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{exog}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{xvar\PYZus{}names} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{results}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{model}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{exog\PYZus{}names}

        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{residual} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{array}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{results}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{resid}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n}{influence} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{results}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{get\PYZus{}influence}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{residual\PYZus{}norm} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{influence}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{resid\PYZus{}studentized\PYZus{}internal}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{leverage} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{influence}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{hat\PYZus{}matrix\PYZus{}diag}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{cooks\PYZus{}distance} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{influence}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{cooks\PYZus{}distance}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{nparams} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{results}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{params}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{nresids} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{residual\PYZus{}norm}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf+fm}{\PYZus{}\PYZus{}call\PYZus{}\PYZus{}}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{plot\PYZus{}context}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{seaborn\PYZhy{}v0\PYZus{}8\PYZhy{}paper}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{o}{*}\PY{o}{*}\PY{n}{kwargs}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}

    \PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{residual\PYZus{}plot}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{ax}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{        }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        Residual vs Fitted Plot}

\PY{l+s+sd}{        Graphical tool to identify non\PYZhy{}linearity.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        (Roughly) Horizontal red line is an indicator that the residual has a linear pattern}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
        \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{ax} \PY{o+ow}{is} \PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{:}
            \PY{n}{fig}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{ax} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{plt}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{subplots}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

        \PY{n}{sns}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{residplot}\PY{p}{(}
            \PY{n}{x}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{y\PYZus{}predict}\PY{p}{,}
            \PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{residual}\PY{p}{,}
            \PY{n}{lowess}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{True}\PY{p}{,}
            \PY{n}{scatter\PYZus{}kws}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{\PYZob{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{alpha}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.5}\PY{p}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{p}{,}
            \PY{n}{line\PYZus{}kws}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{\PYZob{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{color}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{red}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{lw}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{alpha}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{:} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.8}\PY{p}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{p}{,}
            \PY{n}{ax}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{ax}\PY{p}{,}
        \PY{p}{)}
        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} annotations}
        \PY{n}{residual\PYZus{}abs} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{abs}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{residual}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n}{abs\PYZus{}resid} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{flip}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{argsort}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{residual\PYZus{}abs}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n}{abs\PYZus{}resid\PYZus{}top\PYZus{}3} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{abs\PYZus{}resid}\PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{:}\PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{p}{]}
        
        \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{i} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{abs\PYZus{}resid\PYZus{}top\PYZus{}3}\PY{p}{:}
            \PY{n}{ax}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{annotate}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{xy}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{y\PYZus{}predict}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{residual}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{color}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{C3}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
            \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ax.annotate(residual\PYZus{}abs, xy=(self.y\PYZus{}predict[i], self.residual[i]), color=\PYZdq{}C5\PYZdq{})}

        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} for i in abs\PYZus{}resid\PYZus{}top\PYZus{}3:}
        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{}     idx\PYZus{}label = self.residual.index[i]  \PYZsh{} \PYZlt{}\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{} actual df index label}
        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{}     ax.annotate(idx\PYZus{}label, xy=(self.y\PYZus{}predict[i], self.residual[i]), color=\PYZdq{}C3\PYZdq{})}

        \PY{n}{ax}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{set\PYZus{}xlabel}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Fitted values}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n}{ax}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{set\PYZus{}ylabel}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Residuals}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

        \PY{n}{abs\PYZus{}norm\PYZus{}resid\PYZus{}top\PYZus{}3} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{abs\PYZus{}norm\PYZus{}resid}\PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{:}\PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n+nb+bp}{self}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{\PYZus{}\PYZus{}qq\PYZus{}top\PYZus{}resid}\PY{p}{(}
            \PY{n}{QQ}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{theoretical\PYZus{}quantiles}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{abs\PYZus{}norm\PYZus{}resid\PYZus{}top\PYZus{}3}
        \PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
            \PY{n}{ax}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{annotate}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{xy}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{ha}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{right}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{color}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{C3}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}

        \PY{n}{ax}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{set\PYZus{}title}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Normal Q\PYZhy{}Q}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{fontweight}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{bold}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n}{ax}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{set\PYZus{}xlabel}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Theoretical Quantiles}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n}{ax}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{set\PYZus{}ylabel}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Standardized Residuals}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{ax}

    \subsection{Section 1: Data Centre
Segmentation}\label{section-1-data-centre-segmentation}

Recent research by the House Of Commons outlines four DC types (see main
report). The dataset contains several variables that could inform
clustering of DC, but few are uncorrelated with GIA. Since the
interaction terms is used later in the document, the clustering and
independent variable should not come from the same source as GIA, the
planned independent variable. We have tried a variety of combinations of
the clustering based on technical parameters, but these clusters
performed poorly in regression with interaction terms.

\textbf{For this reason, spatial clustring variable waas introduced
using DBSCAN. To identify distinct groups, a combination of elbow method
and visual analysis was used.}

    We observe clusters based on location density:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Cluster 0: Central London.} Most observations, sufficient for
  regression.
\item
  \textbf{Clusters 1,2,3,-1.} Insufficient observations, high chance of
  cluster loss when overlapping with x/y variables.
\end{itemize}

To preserve the most number of datapoints, we joined the non-central
clusters together.

    \subsection{Exploring the Link Between Data Centre Size and Direct
Employment
Generated}\label{exploring-the-link-between-data-centre-size-and-direct-employment-generated}

In this section, we perform regression analysis. Below are some aspects
that determine the choice of variables and methods:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The theme is not well-researched, and there are no assumptions on what
  can drive employment levels in DC. Basic assumption in this study is
  that the larger the facility is (\textbf{b8\_gia\_sqm}), the more
  staff it requires;
\item
  The dataset has gaps, and to study the size-employment link, we need
  to drop the null values in both columns, as well as the outliers.
\item
  Given the uneven N of observations in clusters, some of them will be
  underrepresented, but this was addressed in the clustering section.
\end{itemize}

    \subsubsection{Baseline modelling}\label{baseline-modelling}

    Relevant distributions have long right tails with severe outliers. Due
to the low number of observations, we are applying a sequential approach
to modelling, and aiming to have 1 explanatory variable per model.
Outliers are identified and dropped individually for the model to
maximise the number of datapoints. For the baseline OLS, the outliers
are giving too much variance, which results in statistically
insignificant slope and intercept. We will aim to address them as part
of the Interaction Term model.

    In univariate OLS screening, \textbf{only b8\_gia\_1000\_sqm showed a
statistically significant relationship with total staff numbers}. Other
candidate predictors were not significant, and had substantially lower
explanatory power, so they were excluded from further diagnostics and
modelling.

    \begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi})}
\tightlist
\item
  Residuals vs Fitted plot: potential nonlinearity, the model is
  systematically wrong in the mid fitted range.
\item
  Q-Q plot: positive residuals on the higher end.
\item
  Scale--Location: non-constant variance (heteroskedasticity):
\item
  Residuals vs Leverage: only a few high-leverage points.
\end{enumerate}

For every +1000 sqm of GIA, staffing increases by about +2.3 FTE
(roughly between +1.4 and +3.2, depending on uncertainty method). GIA
explains \textasciitilde48\% of variation in staff numbers. Across all
three models the relationship is consistent. The slope is strongly
statistically significant in both the HC3 model and the robust model,
meaning larger buildings reliably tend to have more staff. The robust
regression gives almost the same result as OLS, suggesting the fitted
relationship isn't being driven by a small number of extreme
observations.

        \PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,}

    The residuals are positive on the north bank, and negative on the south
bank of the River Thames. This might be a coincidence, or it might be
related to differences in fiber network quality in different parts of
London.

Term}\label{linear-regression-with-interaction-term}

    Staffing increases strongly with building size: in the Central group,
staff increases by \textasciitilde2.6 FTE per additional 1,000 sqm.
There is no statistically reliable evidence that Fringe sites have a
different baseline staffing level or a different staffing--GIA slope,
and the robust versions give the same conclusions, suggesting results
are not driven by outliers or heteroskedasticity.

    \begin{center}
    \adjustimage{max size={0.9\linewidth}{0.9\paperheight}}{CASA0007_project_technical_annex_files/CASA0007_project_technical_annex_37_0.png}
    \end{center}
    { \hspace*{\fill} \\}
    
    Compared with the earlier non-interaction models, the main message is
consistent: \textbf{staffing increases as GIA increases}, at roughly
\textbf{\textasciitilde2.2--2.6 FTE per additional 1,000 sqm} (simple
model slope ≈ 2.24; interaction model Central slope ≈ 2.62). The
interaction model improves explanatory power (R² rises from
\textbf{0.46} to \textbf{0.57}), suggesting that allowing for cluster
differences helps capture some extra variation, but the \textbf{Fringe
intercept and Fringe×GIA interaction terms are not statistically
significant}, so there is \textbf{no strong evidence} that Fringe sites
follow a meaningfully different staffing--size relationship than Central
sites in this sample. Taken together, both models imply that \textbf{GIA
is the dominant driver of staffing}, while cluster effects appear
secondary and may be difficult to estimate precisely given the small
number of observations.
